I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this way if you weren't feeling tired after your late shifts?\n\nPatient: Um... hard to say. I'm pretty much always tired after work, so... I don't really know. I guess I feel pretty ashamed afterward. Kind of disgusted with myself, honestly. I've just... I've always thought of myself as a disciplined guy, you know? Dependable. So... doing that, sneaking around my own garage to eat... it just feels like I'm really letting myself down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control while you're eating all that food on those late nights?\n\nPatient: Um... like I was just saying... no. I don't. \n\nIt's... it's just like that switch I mentioned. My brain is telling me to quit, but... I just keep going anyway. So... no. Not really in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating this way every time you have a late shift?\n\nPatient: Um... not every time, no. But... yeah, for the last few months, it's been... maybe once a week. Sometimes two or three times, if it's been a rough day on the line or... you know, a late shift. So... yeah. Often enough, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a sense of relief or comfort while you're eating on those late nights?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I haven't really... paid attention to that. \n\nI guess I don't really think about how I'm feeling when it's happening. I'm just... doing it. So... I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this way if you weren't feeling tired after your late shifts?\n\nPatient: Um... hard to say. I'm pretty much always tired after work, so... I don't really know. I haven't thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this way if you weren't tired after your late shifts, or is the tiredness triggering something else that leads to eating?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I just said, I'm pretty much always beat after a shift. So I don't really have anything to compare it to. \n\nI don't know about it triggering anything else. I just... I get home, and it happens. I haven't really analyzed it beyond that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this way if you weren't alone when you got home from your late shifts?\n\nPatient: Well... probably not, no. \n\nI... I usually make sure I'm alone. Like, even at dinner with my wife, I'll... act like I'm just not that hungry. And then later on, I'll go out to the truck, or... or just wait until she's busy doing something else. \n\nI just... I'd be embarrassed for her to see how much I'm putting away. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this way if you weren't feeling any emotional upset or stress at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I don't really... think about it like that. \n\nI'm not really an emotional guy, so... I couldn't tell you. Work is just work, you know? I haven't really noticed if stress has anything to do with it. I haven't really... paid attention to that. \n\nI guess I don't really think about how I'm feeling when it's happening. I'm just... doing it. So... I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this way if you weren't feeling tired after your late shifts?\n\nPatient: Um... hard to say. I'm pretty much always tired after work, so... I don't really know. I haven't thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this way if you weren't tired after your late shifts, or is the tiredness triggering something else that leads to eating?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I just said, I'm pretty much always beat after a shift. So I don't really have anything to compare it to. \n\nI don't know about it triggering anything else. I just... I get home, and it happens. I haven't really analyzed it beyond that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this way if you weren't alone when you got home from your late shifts?\n\nPatient: Well... probably not, no. \n\nI... I usually make sure I'm alone. Like, even at dinner with my wife, I'll... act like I'm just not that hungry. And then later on, I'll go out to the truck, or... or just wait until she's busy doing something else. \n\nI just... I'd be embarrassed for her to see how much I'm putting away. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this way if you weren't feeling any emotional upset or stress at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I don't really... think about it like that. \n\nI'm not really an emotional guy, so... I couldn't tell you. Work is just work, you know? I haven't really noticed if stress has anything to do with it. Like I said, it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel physically hungry when you start eating on those late nights?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... maybe sometimes? But usually, no. I'm just... eating. I don't really pay attention to whether my stomach is actually growling or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat this way if you weren't feeling tired or anything else, but were feeling upset or anxious?\n\nPatient: I... I really couldn't say. Like I told you, I'm just not one to get all worked up or anxious about things. So I don't know what I'd do in that situation. It's just not something that happens to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't know, once I start, it just feels like a switch gets flipped, I guess. I'll even be sitting there telling myself, you know, 'Roy, just shut the bag and go inside.' I guess I feel pretty ashamed afterward. Kind of disgusted with myself, honestly. I've just... I've always thought of myself as a disciplined guy, you know? Dependable. So... doing that, sneaking around my own garage to eat... it just feels like I'm really letting myself down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control while you're eating all that food on those late nights?\n\nPatient: Um... like I was just saying... no. I don't. \n\nIt's... it's just like that switch I mentioned. My brain is telling me to quit, but... I just keep going anyway. So... no. But... yeah, for the last few months, it's been... maybe once a week. Sometimes two or three times, if it's been a rough day on the line or... you know, a late shift. So... yeah.
